Differential parity cases, device path against the CPU reference (GV-003 "Match reference decisions and verifier outcomes", scenarios "Wide restriction bits are changed", "Old epoch is presented as current" and "Policy batch fabricates an allowance"; GV-001 for the launch attribution; Task 4)
Bend version: bend 2.0.39 (driver sha256 2ec160fa4486d33aff51e9eedcc294d6b531dc10c279c00b0c00685dae2045cd)
Compiler: clang version 23.1.1 at /usr/bin/clang (GPU build: emitted C with -DBEND_CUDA=1 at -O3, per verification/gpu-toolchain.txt); NVRTC 13.4.92 compiles each device program; compute-sanitizer 2026.3.0.0 is the launch observer
Host: Linux 7.2.9-1-MANJARO x86_64, 32 online CPUs; 1-minute load average 79.61 at build time and 49.36 to 60.07 in the runs' load_avg lines; the display and other graphics and compute clients share the GPU; every wall time is indicative only and is not a performance measurement
GPU: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090, GPU-0188dad3-4d11-4254-3a0d-50e3808bf464, bus 00000000:0C:00.0, compute capability 8.9 (cubins sm=89), driver 615.71.09, CUDA UMD 13.4
Date: 2026-11-05 04:14 to 04:21 UTC (2026-11-04 CST)
Environment: export PATH="$HOME/.local/share/writ-toolchains/bend-2.0.39/bend/bin:/opt/cuda/bin:$PATH"; export BEND_NO_TELEMETRY=1; export CUDA_HOME=/opt/cuda. CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES unset and BENDRTS unset in every run (every record states it). Commands run from the worktree root. The probes were uncommitted at capture (each record's lane_commit is b0d40dd marked "uncommitted: M scripts/gpu-validation/..."); their sha256 in every record equals the committed files: Solver.bend 823ab04b7cfb66b6546b93c695c980009bc16840b62869e21a91d9c39d2afd1e, Labels.bend 4bb420f15e931b642c35045c1f783f6d11ff07c2bf736ac1a4a04fe65a0a6455, Transfers.bend 9f15ea73281fc9040ed68a388a35397a78da15babcda3f17271135adfc6c468d.
Execution: device runs, observed: the observed (compute-sanitizer) pass of every observe.sh run below, `+RTS --gpu on -RTS`, with bend_dev launches counted from its sanitizer log. Device runs, not individually observed: the plain pass of every observe.sh run (same binary, options and arguments). CPU runs: every `--gpu off` comparison run, which runs each bang on the CPU pool; the CPU reference inside every run (Core.solve_cpu, Dlm.batch and Transfer.batch without `!`). The cases solver/empty and solver/invalid-flow dispatch nothing to the device in any run: they have CPU coverage only.

Probes: scripts/gpu-validation/Solver.bend, Labels.bend and Transfers.bend gain mode `parity CASE [ROUNDS]` (CASE is `all` or one case name; ROUNDS, default 1, repeats the device call on the same case, each repeat behind its own IO.now() step). Run mode `run SIZE [ROUNDS]` is unchanged except that Solver.device now takes the round budget (run mode passes 0n, as before) and Transfers.hash is now defined over every field in source (the Task 3 review's finding 1: every Plan field and the origins' approved codes and payment rules). The definition closes that finding, but the digest was not exercised in this task: parity mode does not call it, and no `run` invocation of the Task 4 transfers binary was made (see Limits). The Task 3 records stay bound to the earlier probe files by their hashes. One GpuLibrary entry and one bang site per binary (BANGS 1 each, below). All inputs are SYNTHETIC and are marked so in the output.

Per case, each probe runs the device path, then an IO step, then the CPU reference on the same immutable inputs, and prints one PASS or FAIL line for each check:
- `parity CASE`: every device result equals the CPU reference field by field. A difference prints `FAIL parity CASE: FIELD (device X, CPU Y)`. The line passes only if the typed comparison (Solver.result_equal, Dlm.answers_equal, Transfers.Decisions.equal) and a field-by-field walk both find no difference. The fields walked:
  - solver: outcome; failure class (InvalidGraph, TypeConflict, IterationLimit, ...); the candidate stamp's graph, policy and authority epochs, lo and hi words; node count; per node in order, the type domain and the lo and hi words of secrecy, untrusted and effects; the sweep count;
  - labels: the tree shape at every position and every leaf's allowed value;
  - transfers: the tree shape; per leaf the outcome and denial reason; for each admitted ticket every Plan field (stamp epochs, invocation, ordered sources, destination, code, kind, output type) and every OutputPolicy field (schema, secrecy, untrusted, allowed kinds, origin count, and per retained origin its object, secrecy, untrusted, allowed kinds, approved codes and payment rule: the ticket's provenance).
- `expected CASE`: the CPU reference and every device result equal a result stated with the case, so a shared error on both paths still fails.
- `verify CASE` (and `stale CASE` for the solver), on the ORIGINAL inputs:
  - solver: Core.verify on the original Core.Graph, never the solver's work tree, approves each device candidate under the Scenario stamp and refuses it with StaleSnapshot under a newer authority epoch (3 -> 4). A SolveFailed case has no candidate; its line says "not applicable" and is not a PASS;
  - labels: Dlm.verify_batch on the original queries and snapshot;
  - transfers: Transfer.admit per original Plan against its own Context.
- A comparator control per probe: two results differing in one field must be named by the field walk with exactly that field and both values, and refused by the typed comparison. The solver control clears node 2's secrecy lo word, the labels control flips one leaf, the transfers control changes a retained origin's payment rule. Each control exercises one field only (see Limits). The transfers control's expected text has two spaces after "device" and "CPU" ("device  free, CPU  charge 42 for 4") because Transfers.payment renders a leading space. That is intended, and normalizing it would break the control. The note is kept here, not as a probe comment, so the probe's hash stays bound to these records.
The full last device result, the CPU reference and the expected result are printed for every case, so the cross-process `--gpu off` stdout diff below compares every field of every case.

Harness: scripts/gpu-validation/observe.sh and check-record.sh, unchanged since Task 2 (sha256 efd08afb...a8ac and 7d4df17b...88ab in every record). The sanitizer form is the controller's ruling: `compute-sanitizer --dump-kernel-launches --print-limit 0 --kernel-name-exclude regex=.* <binary> +RTS --gpu on -RTS <args>`, plus observe.sh's `--print-session-details --log-file`. Each observe.sh run writes a plain/observed record pair inline below between `--- record v1 ---` and `--- end record ---`. The plain record's `exit_status` is the probe's, the observed record's is compute-sanitizer's, and the `[exit status]` after observe.sh is check-record.sh's gate verdict (0 PASS, 1 FAIL). Per the controller ruling, a case counts as device-validated only with at least one attributed bend_dev launch in a gate-PASS run. Launch counts per case come from the single-case runs (`parity CASE`), because one process gives one sanitizer log and the host backtraces do not name the case. The `parity all` runs are the brief's whole-probe runs; their counts equal the sum of the single-case counts.

## Build
Each probe is built twice from the worktree root to ../writ-build/physical-gpu/task4/ (a new directory, so the Task 3 binaries and their recorded hashes are untouched): first to C (for the BANGS line and emitted_c_sha256), then to the binary, whose build runs the GPU compile and writes NAME.gpu beside it.

## Result
On this host and configuration only (device GPU-0188dad3-4d11-4254-3a0d-50e3808bf464, driver 615.71.09, bend 2.0.39, NVRTC/CUDA 13.4.92; solver.gpu db3438f7ad2d6c9a283fb25c30b67adcbc83e50949db91c30b255dcbeeba7bfb, labels.gpu a3b6885c1fbf98661f0e0712b4fe990ec9b845c87c6732e85c16ca92673af2a1, transfers.gpu aaad38b58edc2fbf38b6fe901b44d9f592db255bd5c9330388f6597628441f0e).
(Task 9 final round, transcription fix, no rerun: the solver.gpu hash on the line above was printed with 65 hex characters; it is corrected to the file on disk and to the records' gpu_program_sha256: `$ sha256sum ../writ-build/physical-gpu/task4/solver.gpu` -> `db3438f7ad2d6c9a283fb25c30b67adcbc83e50949db91c30b255dcbeeba7bfb  ../writ-build/physical-gpu/task4/solver.gpu`.)

Whole-probe runs (`parity all`): solver 90 launches (= 15+15+15+10+15+0+5+0+5+10, the sum of the single-case counts), gate PASS, PASS 35 FAIL 0; labels 20 launches (4 x 5), gate PASS, PASS 13 FAIL 0; transfers 30 launches (6 x 5), gate PASS, PASS 19 FAIL 0. The gate PASS of `solver parity all` does not validate the empty and invalid-flow cases on the device; they contribute 0 launches there too.

Comparison:
- Every listed case printed `PASS parity CASE` under `--gpu on` (plain and observed passes) and under `--gpu off`. No `FAIL` line appears in any run.
- In all 24 runs, the `--gpu off` stdout is byte-identical (diff empty) to both the `--gpu on` plain-pass stdout and the observed-pass stdout. Every run prints every field of the last device result and of the CPU reference for every case, so this is a field-by-field comparison across processes as well as in process.
- Every `expected` line, every `verify` line and every solver `stale` line passed, and all three comparator controls passed in every run. The SolveFailed cases (type-conflict, invalid-flow, budget-1) print "verify ...: not applicable", as designed.
- No device result differed from a CPU result. There is no finding of a difference to record.
- Re-check: check-record.sh on the 24 stored records returns 0 for the 21 gate-PASS pairs and 1 for the three FAIL pairs (solver-empty and solver-invalid-flow: 0 launches; transfers-paired-admitted: presence).

Device-validated cases: 18 of the 20 listed cases (all except solver/empty and solver/invalid-flow, which dispatch nothing by construction), counting transfers/paired-admitted through its ROUNDS 400 run. The single-round transfers/paired-admitted run is recorded as refused by the gate on presence, with its 5 observed launches. Per the controller ruling it was rerun only through the ROUNDS argument, which repeats the same device batch on the same inputs without changing the case.

The independent four-node oracle (PropertyTests.bend, 320 cases) is not copied here; it runs inside the corpus in Task 6. It is finite coverage of its documented cases, not of arbitrary graphs or sizes. The same holds for these 20 cases.

## Limits
- One host, one GPU, one driver, one Bend and CUDA version. The display and other compute clients shared the GPU. Load average was 49 to 60 during the runs. Wall times are indicative only.
- Finite, small, synthetic cases (2 to 4 nodes or leaves): no large graphs, no adversarial policy sizes, no delegation (acts-for) edges in the label snapshot, no multi-source transfers, no Copy or Transform operations, and no InvalidPolicy, Source, Schema, Operation, Origin or Destination denials. In the solver, IterationLimit is shown only for budget 1. The four-node oracle is Task 6's.
- No device coverage: solver/empty and solver/invalid-flow return before any sweep, so their CPU-path parity is the only evidence for them.
- The label and transfer `verify` lines replay the same evaluators as the CPU reference (Dlm.verify_batch is Dlm.batch plus answers_equal; the transfer replay is Transfer.admit, which Transfer.batch calls). Nothing in this task shows them refusing a wrong candidate; that is Task 5's injected faults. Core.verify is a separate algorithm, and its StaleSnapshot refusal is shown for every Solved solver case. The comparator controls show that the field walks and typed comparisons detect a one-field change. They are not a test of the device.
- Each comparator control exercises one field: solver node 2's secrecy lo word, labels one leaf, transfers one origin's payment rule. Every other walked field (stamp epochs, node count, type domains, the other restriction words, sweeps, failure class, tree shape, denial reason, the Plan fields and the other OutputPolicy and origin fields) is covered by reading the walks and by the typed equality, not by a per-field control.
- The revised all-field Transfers.hash is compiled into the Task 4 transfers binary, but no Task 4 run executes it. It is reached only from run mode (Transfers.digest and Transfers.tally), and no `run` invocation was recorded here. So nothing in this file shows that it runs, that device and CPU agree on it, or that it distinguishes tickets differing in one of the added fields. The Task 3 run-mode records stay bound to the earlier probe file, whose digest was partial. Task 6's corpus run does not reach it either, because the corpus does not run this probe. A `transfers run SIZE` invocation of the Task 4 binary would exercise it, and a hash-sensitivity control can join Task 5's injected faults. Neither was run now.
- The independent checks run in the same process and address space as the device result. They are a logical check, not a protection boundary.
- Hidden-device runs (an invalid CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES id), the default `--gpu` mode and `--gpu <size>` were not run for parity mode. Device memory checking was not run (the sanitizer excludes every kernel by design), and Nsight is absent.
- The sampler evidence is thin. Only the ROUNDS 400 run lasted long enough for a numeric per-PID pmon sample (max SM 43). The other runs are under 10 s, where the gate does not use the samplers.
- Pre-commit capture: every record's lane_commit is b0d40dd marked uncommitted for the three probes. Their source_sha256 values bind the records to the committed files listed in the header.
- Not run: `bend Tests.bend` and `bend PROOF.bend`. No library, law or proof source changed; only scripts/gpu-validation/*.bend.
